{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WfmHDWwE18FeNN7qgFxnud","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0009 — Implementation notes\n2\t\n3\t## Design choices\n4\t\n5\t1. **Tuples instead of lists.** A frozen dataclass with mutable list\n6\t   defaults would be either (a) unhashable, or (b) a foot-gun if a\n7\t   caller passed in a shared list. `tuple` plus\n8\t   `field(default_factory=tuple)` gives an immutable, hashable report\n9\t   while remaining trivially JSON-serialisable (json.dumps converts\n10\t   tuples to arrays anyway, and `to_json()` materialises lists\n11\t   explicitly for clarity).\n12\t\n13\t2. **Why a dedicated `CapabilityStatus` instead of `dict`s?**\n14\t   `report[]` carries structured per-capability outcomes that the\n15\t   doctor renders into a green checklist (design-spec §11). Using a\n16\t   dataclass:\n17\t   - matches the pattern set by `Capability` (T01.09) and `EvalSpec`\n18\t     (T01.03),\n19\t   - documents the row shape at the type level rather than in\n20\t     freeform dicts,\n21\t   - lets `to_json()` produce stable key ordering via an explicit\n22\t     `to_dict()` method instead of relying on Python dict insertion\n23\t     order from arbitrary call sites.\n24\t\n25\t3. **`to_json()` returns `dict`, not `str`.** DM-008 says \"serializable\n26\t   to JSON\" and the validation report (L2 patch) demoted byte-level\n27\t   determinism to a Notes-level concern. Returning a mapping lets\n28\t   callers compose it (e.g. doctor wrapping it in a larger envelope)\n29\t   without re-parsing.\n30\t\n31\t4. **`skipped_by_flag` field on `CapabilityStatus`.** Distinguishes\n32\t   \"capability genuinely failed\" from \"capability passed but the user\n33\t   asked us to ignore it (`--no-mcp`)\". COMP-009 (T01.11) sets this\n34\t   bit when it sees a `Capability.skip_flag` in the active skip set.\n35\t\n36\t5. **Key ordering.** `to_json()` emits keys in dataclass-field order,\n37\t   not alphabetical, so the JSON layout mirrors how a reader would read\n38\t   the dataclass definition. This makes diffs in doctor snapshot tests\n39\t   meaningful (see T01.13 Notes about derived determinism).\n40\t\n41\t## Trade-offs and follow-ups\n42\t\n43\t- **No reverse `from_dict()`.** DM-008 only requires serialisation,\n44\t  not round-tripping. If a future test asserts JSON → `CapabilityReport`\n45\t  parity we can add `from_json()` then; right now it would be\n46\t  speculative.\n47\t- **Tuple ordering is caller-controlled.** `CapabilityGates.check_all`\n48\t  (T01.11) is responsible for emitting capabilities in the canonical\n49\t  `CAPABILITIES` order, which is what gives the doctor output its\n50\t  stable shape. `CapabilityReport` does not sort defensively.\n51\t- **`Capability` not used as a `report[]` entry.** A `Capability`\n52\t  holds a `Callable`, which is not JSON-safe. Hence the dedicated\n53\t  `CapabilityStatus` value type.\n54\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"293563b4-1ea3-4bc4-aee5-9bab90f1f262","uuid":"e47134c7-6be6-47ea-96a3-da9fa227c94b","timestamp":"2026-05-20T05:35:23.546Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0009/notes.md","content":"# D-0009 — Implementation notes\n\n## Design choices\n\n1. **Tuples instead of lists.** A frozen dataclass with mutable list\n   defaults would be either (a) unhashable, or (b) a foot-gun if a\n   caller passed in a shared list. `tuple` plus\n   `field(default_factory=tuple)` gives an immutable, hashable report\n   while remaining trivially JSON-serialisable (json.dumps converts\n   tuples to arrays anyway, and `to_json()` materialises lists\n   explicitly for clarity).\n\n2. **Why a dedicated `CapabilityStatus` instead of `dict`s?**\n   `report[]` carries structured per-capability outcomes that the\n   doctor renders into a green checklist (design-spec §11). Using a\n   dataclass:\n   - matches the pattern set by `Capability` (T01.09) and `EvalSpec`\n     (T01.03),\n   - documents the row shape at the type level rather than in\n     freeform dicts,\n   - lets `to_json()` produce stable key ordering via an explicit\n     `to_dict()` method instead of relying on Python dict insertion\n     order from arbitrary call sites.\n\n3. **`to_json()` returns `dict`, not `str`.** DM-008 says \"serializable\n   to JSON\" and the validation report (L2 patch) demoted byte-level\n   determinism to a Notes-level concern. Returning a mapping lets\n   callers compose it (e.g. doctor wrapping it in a larger envelope)\n   without re-parsing.\n\n4. **`skipped_by_flag` field on `CapabilityStatus`.** Distinguishes\n   \"capability genuinely failed\" from \"capability passed but the user\n   asked us to ignore it (`--no-mcp`)\". COMP-009 (T01.11) sets this\n   bit when it sees a `Capability.skip_flag` in the active skip set.\n\n5. **Key ordering.** `to_json()` emits keys in dataclass-field order,\n   not alphabetical, so the JSON layout mirrors how a reader would read\n   the dataclass definition. This makes diffs in doctor snapshot tests\n   meaningful (see T01.13 Notes about derived determinism).\n\n## Trade-offs and follow-ups\n\n- **No reverse `from_dict()`.** DM-008 only requires serialisation,\n  not round-tripping. If a future test asserts JSON → `CapabilityReport`\n  parity we can add `from_json()` then; right now it would be\n  speculative.\n- **Tuple ordering is caller-controlled.** `CapabilityGates.check_all`\n  (T01.11) is responsible for emitting capabilities in the canonical\n  `CAPABILITIES` order, which is what gives the doctor output its\n  stable shape. `CapabilityReport` does not sort defensively.\n- **`Capability` not used as a `report[]` entry.** A `Capability`\n  holds a `Callable`, which is not JSON-safe. Hence the dedicated\n  `CapabilityStatus` value type.\n","numLines":54,"startLine":1,"totalLines":54}}}

We intentionally do not make\n# the three concrete classes inherit from a new base — the per-class\n# subclassing already keeps them disjoint, and a base class would force\n# downstream callers (and existing tests) to deal with a new ABC.\nSuiteLoaderError = (SchemaError, InvalidEvalId, UnresolvedCapability)\n\n\n@runtime_checkable\nclass CapabilityResolver(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Pluggable contract the SuiteLoader uses to gate ``requires`` clauses.\n\n    The Protocol intentionally has a single method so the M1 SuiteLoader\n    can be wired before COMP-009 ``CapabilityGates`` (T01.11) lands. The\n    real implementation arriving in T01.11 inspects PATH + MCP reachability\n    + ``--no-mcp`` flags and either returns the set of unresolved\n    capability names (which the loader turns into\n    :class:`UnresolvedCapability`) or returns an empty iterable.\n\n    Test code injects a stub that records its calls so the \"id-check\n    happens BEFORE capability resolution\" acceptance criterion can be\n    verified by call-order assertion rather than by side-effects.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the subset of ``required`` that could not be satisfied.\n\n        Implementations MUST be pure with respect to the SuiteLoader\n        invariants: no filesystem writes, no globals touched. Returning\n        an empty iterable means every capability is satisfied; any\n        non-empty iterable is bubbled up by the loader as\n        :class:`UnresolvedCapability`.\n        \"\"\"\n        ...\n\n\nclass PermissiveCapabilityResolver:\n    \"\"\"Default :class:`CapabilityResolver` that approves every requirement.\n\n    Used by :class:`SuiteLoader` when the caller does not inject a real\n    resolver — tests and ``eval doctor``'s dry runs both benefit from a\n    no-op default. The real ``CapabilityGates`` implementation lands in\n    T01.11 and is wired through ``commands.py`` (T01.13).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def resolve(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        required: tuple[str, ...],\n    ) -> Iterable[str]:\n        return ()\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ParsedSuite:\n    \"\"\"Schema-validated, capability-checked, parameterize-expanded suite.\n\n    The :class:`SuiteLoader.load` return shape. Every field is a verbatim\n    projection of the manifest envelope except ``evals``, which holds the\n    *expanded* eval list (parameterize rows materialised into one\n    ``EvalSpec`` per row, with ``.{index}``-suffixed ids).\n\n    Sequence fields are tuples so the dataclass is hashable and downstream\n    consumers cannot mutate them through the container.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    description: str\n    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n    source_path: Path\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass SuiteLoader:\n    \"\"\"COMP-002 orchestrator: turn a manifest path into a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n    The gate ordering is fixed and documented in\n    ``artifacts/D-0006/spec.md`` (T01.07):\n\n    1. **Read + schema** (``_validate_manifest_dict``) — FR-SCH1.\n    2. **Static id regex** (``validate_eval_id`` on every ``evals[].id``) —\n       FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1. Runs BEFORE capability resolution so a\n       traversal-pattern id cannot trigger ``shutil.which`` calls or\n       network MCP-reachability probes.\n    3. **Capability resolution** (the injected :class:`CapabilityResolver`)\n       — COMP-009 contract surface.\n    4. **Parameterize expansion** — generate ``E2.1, E2.2, ...`` from\n       ``parameterize:`` rows, base-id suffixed by ``.{index}``.\n    5. **Expanded-id regex re-check** (``validate_eval_id`` again) —\n       the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application that closes the\n       expansion-time path-traversal surface.\n\n    Every typed error (``SchemaError``, ``InvalidEvalId``,\n    ``UnresolvedCapability``) is allowed to propagate as-is so CLI\n    callers map it to :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) and\n    emit the error class name on stderr — operators see *which* gate\n    fired without us collapsing the failure classes.\n\n    The class is intentionally a plain dataclass with one composed\n    field (the resolver) rather than a long ``__init__`` parameter list,\n    so future T01.14 (ExpectDSL) wiring can be added as additional\n    optional fields without breaking call sites.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    capability_resolver: CapabilityResolver = field(\n        default_factory=PermissiveCapabilityResolver\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def load(self, path: Path | str) -> ParsedSuite:\n        \"\"\"Run the five-stage gate chain and return a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n        Args:\n            path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str``\n                accepted for ergonomics; normalised to ``Path``.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`ParsedSuite` whose ``evals`` tuple holds the\n            post-parameterize-expansion eval list. Suite-level metadata\n            is verbatim from the manifest envelope so callers do not\n            need to re-read the file to access ``required_binaries``,\n            ``defaults``, etc.\n\n        Raises:\n            SchemaError: Manifest read / YAML decode / jsonschema\n                violation. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`.\n            InvalidEvalId: A static or post-expansion eval id failed\n                :func:`validate_eval_id`. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`. Raised BEFORE any\n                capability resolution call (verified by mocked test).\n            UnresolvedCapability: The injected\n                :class:`CapabilityResolver` rejected one or more\n                ``requires`` entries. Exit-mapped via\n                :data:`UNRESOLVED_CAPABILITY_EXIT_CODE`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        manifest_path = Path(path)\n\n        # Stage 1+2: schema and read. Re-uses validate_manifest's\n        # ingress so any FR-SCH1 rejection flows through one code path.\n        manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n        raw_evals = manifest[\"evals\"]\n\n        # Stage 3: FR-SCH2 static id guard. Apply to every manifest id\n        # BEFORE we touch the capability resolver — see the spec.md\n        # \"ordering rationale\" section for why this matters\n        # (CapabilityGates can shell out via shutil.which / MCP probes).\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            validate_eval_id(entry.get(\"id\"))\n\n        # Stage 4: capability resolution. Each eval's ``requires`` list\n        # is funnelled through the injected resolver; the first eval\n        # with unresolved capabilities short-circuits the load.\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            self._check_capabilities(entry)\n\n        # Stage 5: parameterize expansion + post-expansion id re-check.\n        # Static ids without ``parameterize`` are passed through; ids\n        # with ``parameterize`` produce one expanded EvalSpec per row.\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            expanded.extend(self._expand_entry(entry))\n\n        return ParsedSuite(\n            name=manifest[\"name\"],\n            version=manifest[\"version\"],\n            description=manifest[\"description\"],\n            defaults=manifest.get(\"defaults\", {}),\n            required_binaries=tuple(manifest.get(\"required_binaries\", ())),\n            optional_capabilities=tuple(manifest.get(\"optional_capabilities\", ())),\n            evals=tuple(expanded),\n            source_path=manifest_path,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _check_capabilities(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Funnel one eval's ``requires`` clause through the resolver.\"\"\"\n\n        required = tuple(entry.get(\"requires\", ()))\n        missing = tuple(self.capability_resolver.resolve(entry[\"id\"], required))\n        if missing:\n            raise UnresolvedCapability(eval_id=entry[\"id\"], missing=missing)\n\n    def _expand_entry(self, entry: Mapping[str, Any]) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise ``parameterize`` rows into one ``EvalSpec`` per row.\n\n        Expansion convention (design-spec §5):\n\n        * Base id with no ``parameterize`` → one ``EvalSpec`` with the\n          static id unchanged.\n        * Base id with N ``parameterize`` rows → N ``EvalSpec`` rows\n          whose ids are ``{base}.{1..N}``.\n\n        Each expanded id is re-checked against :func:`validate_eval_id`\n        before it returns — this is the load-bearing FR-SCH2 application\n        that the security model relies on. The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. The\n        loader's job is to preserve the row data on the expanded\n        ``EvalSpec`` (currently round-tripped via ``EvalSpec.from_dict``\n        keeping ``parameterize`` populated) so the runner can apply\n        substitutions per-id.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        parameterize_rows = entry.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n        if not parameterize_rows:\n            return [EvalSpec.from_dict(entry)]\n\n        expanded: list[EvalSpec] = []\n        base_id = entry[\"id\"]\n        for index, _row in enumerate(parameterize_rows, start=1):\n            expanded_id = f\"{base_id}.{index}\"\n            # FR-SCH2 re-check — see method docstring for rationale.\n            validate_eval_id(expanded_id)\n            expanded_entry = dict(entry)\n            expanded_entry[\"id\"] = expanded_id\n            expanded.append(EvalSpec.from_dict(expanded_entry))\n        return expanded\n","numLines":624,"startLine":1,"totalLines":624}}}

Concurrency model\n717\t\n718\t### Default: `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)`\n719\t\n720\tThe orchestrator uses `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (matching `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`'s pattern, NOT the heavier `execution/parallel.py`). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n721\t\n722\t### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n723\t\n724\t1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n725\t2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n726\t3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n727\t4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n728\t\n729\t### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n730\t\n731\tFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n732\t\n733\t### Signal handling\n734\t\n735\t- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n736\t- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n737\t- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n738\t\n739\t### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n740\t\n741\t- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n742\t- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n743\t- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n744\t\n745\t---\n746\t\n747\t## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n748\t\n749\t### What we fork\n750\t\n751\t`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` (MIT, Python 3.8+). The repo has 1 star / 0 forks — bus-factor risk is real. Mitigation: **fork-and-own**.\n752\t\n753\t### Vendoring layout\n754\t\n755\t```\n756\tsrc/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/\n757\t├── __init__.py\n758\t├── driver.py        # PtyDriver (was: ptytest/__init__.py PtySession)\n759\t├── stream.py        # ANSI strip + line buffer (was: ptytest/_stream.py)\n760\t├── LICENSE          # upstream MIT, verbatim\n761\t└── PROVENANCE.md    # fork SHA, what we changed, why\n762\t```\n763\t\n764\t### What we change vs upstream\n765\t\n766\t| Change | Reason |\n767\t|---|---|\n768\t| Rename `PtySession` → `PtyDriver` | Avoid pytest-fixture connotation; we use it in a non-pytest context too. |\n769\t| Remove pytest-plugin entry-point | We don't need fixture autoloading. |\n770\t| Add `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=)` method | Cleaner waiting for Claude Code's TTY-prompt signal (the `>` or `$` indicator that input is welcome). |\n771\t| Add `inject_prompt(text)` method | Adds CR-LF + flushes — wraps stdin writes for Claude Code idioms. |\n772\t| Tighten dependency to `pexpect>=4.9` | The vendored ptytest doesn't pin pexpect tightly. |\n773\t| Apply ANSI-strip aggressively | Claude Code emits rich ANSI; the eval's TTY assertions need plain text. |\n774\t\n775\t### What we DON'T change\n776\t\n777\t- Core `pexpect.spawn` mechanics — leave it alone.\n778\t- The Docker-isolation mode (we don't need it; per-eval HOME isolation is enough).\n779\t- The original test suite — we vendor it under `tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py` to catch regressions during future ptytest re-syncs."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"293563b4-1ea3-4bc4-aee5-9bab90f1f262","uuid":"bccf8b7c-ef76-4614-88b4-ffc442bffdea","timestamp":"2026-05-20T05:35:53.123Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/design-spec.md","content":"13 new files under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/` and `cli/eval/pty/` (vendored ptytest). Cannot proceed. |\n| **SOFT-SKIP** | User passed `--no-mcp` or `mcp_server.auggie` check fails | Mark gated evals as SKIPPED with reason. Continue. |\n| **SOFT-XFAIL** | Manifest declares `xfail_if: <condition>` | Run, but expected-fail. Status is XPASS if it passes anyway. |\n\n### Capability check implementation (interface only)\n\n```python\n# cli/eval/capability_gates.py\n\n@dataclass\nclass Capability:\n    name: str\n    check: Callable[[], bool]\n    failure_mode: Literal[\"hard\", \"skip\", \"xfail\"]\n    skip_flag: Optional[str] = None    # CLI flag that forces skip (e.g., --no-mcp)\n    description: str = \"\"\n\nCAPABILITIES = [\n    Capability(\"binary.claude\",  lambda: shutil.which(\"claude\")  is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"binary.make\",    lambda: shutil.which(\"make\")    is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"binary.jq\",      lambda: shutil.which(\"jq\")      is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"binary.git\",     lambda: shutil.which(\"git\")     is not None, \"hard\"),\n    Capability(\"mcp_server.auggie\",            mcp_server_reachable(\"auggie\"),            \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n    Capability(\"mcp_server.auggie-mcp\",        mcp_server_reachable(\"auggie-mcp\"),        \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n    Capability(\"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\", mcp_server_reachable(\"airis-mcp-gateway\"), \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\"),\n]\n\ndef check_all(skip_flags: set[str]) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Run all checks; return per-cap status + which evals are blocked.\"\"\"\n```\n\n### `eval doctor` output\n\n```\n🔧 superclaude eval doctor\n\nHard requirements:\n  ✅ claude:  /usr/local/bin/claude (v0.5.3)\n  ✅ make:    /usr/bin/make\n  ✅ jq:      /usr/bin/jq\n  ✅ git:     /usr/bin/git\n\nOptional capabilities:\n  ✅ mcp_server.auggie:            reachable via stdio\n  ✅ mcp_server.auggie-mcp:        reachable via stdio\n  ❌ mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway: unreachable (1 eval will skip)\n\nVerdict: READY (1 eval will be skipped without --no-mcp override)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 12. Concurrency model\n\n### Default: `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)`\n\nThe orchestrator uses `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (matching `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`'s pattern, NOT the heavier `execution/parallel.py`). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n\n### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n\n1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n\n### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n\nFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n\n### Signal handling\n\n- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n\n### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n\n- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n\n---\n\n## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n\n### What we fork\n\n`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` (MIT, Python 3.8+). The repo has 1 star / 0 forks — bus-factor risk is real. Mitigation: **fork-and-own**.\n\n### Vendoring layout\n\n```\nsrc/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/\n├── __init__.py\n├── driver.py        # PtyDriver (was: ptytest/__init__.py PtySession)\n├── stream.py        # ANSI strip + line buffer (was: ptytest/_stream.py)\n├── LICENSE          # upstream MIT, verbatim\n└── PROVENANCE.md    # fork SHA, what we changed, why\n```\n\n### What we change vs upstream\n\n| Change | Reason |\n|---|---|\n| Rename `PtySession` → `PtyDriver` | Avoid pytest-fixture connotation; we use it in a non-pytest context too. |\n| Remove pytest-plugin entry-point | We don't need fixture autoloading. |\n| Add `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=)` method | Cleaner waiting for Claude Code's TTY-prompt signal (the `>` or `$` indicator that input is welcome). |\n| Add `inject_prompt(text)` method | Adds CR-LF + flushes — wraps stdin writes for Claude Code idioms. |\n| Tighten dependency to `pexpect>=4.9` | The vendored ptytest doesn't pin pexpect tightly. |\n| Apply ANSI-strip aggressively | Claude Code emits rich ANSI; the eval's TTY assertions need plain text. |\n\n### What we DON'T change\n\n- Core `pexpect.spawn` mechanics — leave it alone.\n- The Docker-isolation mode (we don't need it; per-eval HOME isolation is enough).\n- The original test suite — we vendor it under `tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py` to catch regressions during future ptytest re-syncs.","numLines":130,"startLine":650,"totalLines":844}}}

Type alias for the (passed, detail) tuple returned by per-target probes.\nProbeResult = tuple[bool, str]\n\n\nclass CapabilityGates:\n    \"\"\"COMP-009: evaluate every capability and emit a ``CapabilityReport``.\n\n    The gate is constructed once per ``eval doctor`` / ``eval run``\n    invocation. Pass the active CLI ``skip_flags`` (e.g. ``{\"--no-mcp\"}``)\n    so SOFT-SKIP capabilities whose descriptor names a matching flag are\n    forced to skipped status even when their underlying probe would pass.\n\n    The class is purposely small: ``check_all`` drives the static\n    :data:`_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` roster through :meth:`which_or_skip`\n    or :meth:`mcp_server_reachable` and assembles the report. Tests\n    inject a custom spec tuple via the ``capabilities`` keyword to mock\n    PATH state without monkeypatching ``shutil``.\n\n    OQ-5 (roadmap Open Questions): the precise ``mcp_server_reachable``\n    contract (PATH presence vs handshake vs SSE probe) must be resolved\n    before COMP-009 close at M2. The M1 implementation here uses PATH\n    presence as the reachability signal — see :meth:`mcp_server_reachable`\n    for the deferral note and the override hook.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n        capabilities: Optional[Iterable[_CapabilitySpec]] = None,\n        mcp_probe: Optional[Callable[[str], ProbeResult]] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        self._skip_flags: frozenset[str] = frozenset(skip_flags or ())\n        self._specs: tuple[_CapabilitySpec, ...] = tuple(\n            capabilities if capabilities is not None else _DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS\n        )\n        # Test hook so ``mcp_server_reachable`` can be redirected without\n        # monkeypatching the module. Production callers leave this ``None``\n        # and the default PATH-presence probe (OQ-5 M1 stub) is used.\n        self._mcp_probe = mcp_probe\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def skip_flags(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n        \"\"\"Active CLI skip flags, sorted for deterministic report output.\"\"\"\n        return tuple(sorted(self._skip_flags))\n\n    def capabilities(self) -> tuple[Capability, ...]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise the static spec table as ``Capability`` instances.\n\n        Each ``Capability.check`` is a closure that defers to\n        :meth:`which_or_skip` / :meth:`mcp_server_reachable` so the\n        descriptors stay in sync with the gate's own probing — there is no\n        second copy of the lookup logic in the closures.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return tuple(self._make_capability(spec) for spec in self._specs)\n\n    def which_or_skip(self, name: str) -> ProbeResult:\n        \"\"\"Probe whether binary ``name`` is on ``PATH``.\n\n        Returns ``(True, \"<resolved-path>\")`` when ``shutil.which`` finds\n        the binary, ``(False, \"not found on PATH\")`` otherwise. The\n        ``detail`` half is carried verbatim onto the\n        :class:`CapabilityStatus` row so ``eval doctor`` can print the\n        resolved path on success or the failure reason on miss.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        path = shutil.which(name)\n        if path is None:\n            return False, \"not found on PATH\"\n        return True, path\n\n    def mcp_server_reachable(self, name: str) -> ProbeResult:\n        \"\"\"Probe whether MCP server ``name`` is reachable.\n\n        OQ-5 deferral: the canonical reachability semantics (PATH\n        presence vs full stdio handshake vs SSE health probe) are open\n        and must resolve before COMP-009 closes at M2. Until then, this\n        implementation treats \"binary on PATH\" as the reachability\n        signal because every MCP server in the default roster\n        (``auggie``, ``auggie-mcp``, ``airis-mcp-gateway``) ships as an\n        executable.\n\n        Tests inject a custom probe via the ``mcp_probe`` constructor\n        argument; the M2 follow-up will land a real handshake probe\n        under the same signature.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._mcp_probe is not None:\n            return self._mcp_probe(name)\n        path = shutil.which(name)\n        if path is None:\n            return False, \"MCP server binary not on PATH\"\n        return True, path\n\n    def check_all(self) -> CapabilityReport:\n        \"\"\"Run every spec through its probe and assemble the report.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`CapabilityReport` whose ``report`` tuple has one\n            :class:`CapabilityStatus` row per spec in declaration order.\n            ``hard_failures`` / ``soft_skips`` / ``soft_xfails`` carry\n            the capability names that failed (or were skip-flagged) per\n            their declared ``failure_mode``. ``blocked_evals`` is empty\n            here — the SuiteLoader (T01.07) is the layer that knows\n            which evals depend on which capability and populates that\n            list at run time.\n\n            Calling :meth:`check_all` twice on the same gate instance\n            produces equal reports (idempotence is required by the\n            T01.11 acceptance criteria; verified by\n            ``test_check_all_is_idempotent``).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        rows: list[CapabilityStatus] = []\n        hard_failures: list[str] = []\n        soft_skips: list[str] = []\n        soft_xfails: list[str] = []\n\n        for spec in self._specs:\n            passed, detail = self._probe(spec)\n            skipped_by_flag = False\n            if (\n                spec.skip_flag is not None\n                and spec.skip_flag in self._skip_flags\n            ):\n                # SOFT-SKIP override: the user asked us to ignore this\n                # capability tier regardless of probe result. The probe\n                # detail is preserved so doctor output can still show\n                # why the capability would have passed/failed without\n                # the flag.\n                skipped_by_flag = True\n                passed = False\n\n            rows.append(\n                CapabilityStatus(\n                    name=spec.name,\n                    passed=passed,\n                    failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,\n                    description=spec.description,\n                    detail=detail,\n                    skipped_by_flag=skipped_by_flag,\n                )\n            )\n\n            if not passed:\n                if spec.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n                    hard_failures.append(spec.name)\n                elif spec.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n                    soft_skips.append(spec.name)\n                else:  # \"xfail\"\n                    soft_xfails.append(spec.name)\n\n        return CapabilityReport(\n            report=tuple(rows),\n            blocked_evals=(),\n            skip_flags=self.skip_flags,\n            hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n            soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n            soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _probe(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> ProbeResult:\n        if spec.kind == \"binary\":\n            return self.which_or_skip(spec.target)\n        if spec.kind == \"mcp_server\":\n            return self.mcp_server_reachable(spec.target)\n        # Defensive — the Literal annotation prevents this at type-check\n        # time, but the runtime guard keeps a typo in a custom spec\n        # tuple from silently producing an always-passing row.\n        raise ValueError(f\"unknown capability kind: {spec.kind!r}\")\n\n    def _make_capability(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> Capability:\n        if spec.kind == \"binary\":\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.which_or_skip(target)[0]\n        else:\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.mcp_server_reachable(target)[0]\n        return Capability(\n            name=spec.name,\n            check=_check,\n            failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,\n            skip_flag=spec.skip_flag,\n            description=spec.description,\n        )"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":483,"cache_read_input_tokens":131154,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":483,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"293563b4-1ea3-4bc4-aee5-9bab90f1f262","uuid":"e414b666-7e00-41f8-8766-b64fc5f45070"}

---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass _CapabilitySpec:\n    \"\"\"Static descriptor of one capability the harness checks.\n\n rows from a\n    single source of truth without repeating the binary name.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    target: str\n    kind: Literal[\"binary\", \"mcp_server\"]\n    failure_mode: FailureMode\n    skip_flag: Optional[str]\n    description: str\n\n\n# Roster ordering matches design-spec §11 (HARD binaries first, then\n# SOFT-SKIP MCP servers). ``CapabilityGates`` emits ``report[]`` rows in\n# this order, which is what gives ``eval doctor`` (T01.13) its stable\n# green-checklist layout.\n_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS: tuple[_CapabilitySpec, ...] = (\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"binary.claude\", \"claude\", \"binary\", \"hard\", None,\n        \"Claude CLI on PATH\",\n    ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"binary.make\", \"make\", \"binary\", \"hard\", None,\n        \"GNU make on PATH\",\n    ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"binary.jq\", \"jq\", \"binary\", \"hard\", None,\n        \"jq JSON processor on PATH\",\n    ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"binary.git\", \"git\", \"binary\", \"hard\", None,\n        \"git VCS on PATH\",\n    ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"mcp_server.auggie\", \"auggie\", \"mcp_server\", \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\",\n        \"Auggie ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\", \"airis-mcp-gateway\",\n        \"mcp_server\", \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\",\n        \"AIRIS MCP gateway reachable\",\n    ),\n)\n\n\n# Type alias for the (passed, detail) tuple returned by per-target probes.\nProbeResult = tuple[bool, str]\n\n\nclass CapabilityGates:\n    \"\"\"COMP-009: evaluate every capability and emit a ``CapabilityReport``.\n\n    The gate is constructed once per ``eval doctor`` / ``eval run``\n    invocation. Pass the active CLI ``skip_flags`` (e.g. ``{\"--no-mcp\"}``)\n    so SOFT-SKIP capabilities whose descriptor names a matching flag are\n    forced to skipped status even when their underlying probe would pass.\n\n    The class is purposely small: ``check_all`` drives the static\n    :data:`_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` roster through :meth:`which_or_skip`\n    or :meth:`mcp_server_reachable` and assembles the report. Tests\n    inject a custom spec tuple via the ``capabilities`` keyword to mock\n    PATH state without monkeypatching ``shutil``.\n\n    OQ-5 (roadmap Open Questions): the precise ``mcp_server_reachable``\n    contract (PATH presence vs handshake vs SSE probe) must be resolved\n    before COMP-009 close at M2. The M1 implementation here uses PATH\n    presence as the reachability signal — see :meth:`mcp_server_reachable`\n    for the deferral note and the override hook.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n        capabilities: Optional[Iterable[_CapabilitySpec]] = None,\n        mcp_probe: Optional[Callable[[str], ProbeResult]] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        self._skip_flags: frozenset[str] = frozenset(skip_flags or ())\n        self._specs: tuple[_CapabilitySpec, ...] = tuple(\n            capabilities if capabilities is not None else _DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS\n        )\n        # Test hook so ``mcp_server_reachable`` can be redirected without\n        # monkeypatching the module. Production callers leave this ``None``\n        # and the default PATH-presence probe (OQ-5 M1 stub) is used.\n        self._mcp_probe = mcp_probe\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def skip_flags(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n        \"\"\"Active CLI skip flags, sorted for deterministic report output.\"\"\"\n        return tuple(sorted(self._skip_flags))\n\n    def capabilities(self) -> tuple[Capability, ...]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise the static spec table as ``Capability`` instances.\n\n        Each ``Capability.check`` is a closure that defers to\n        :meth:`which_or_skip` / :meth:`mcp_server_reachable` so the\n        descriptors stay in sync with the gate's own probing — there is no\n        second copy of the lookup logic in the closures.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return tuple(self._make_capability(spec) for spec in self._specs)\n\n    def which_or_skip(self, name: str) -> ProbeResult:\n        \"\"\"Probe whether binary ``name`` is on ``PATH``.\n\n        Returns ``(True, \"<resolved-path>\")`` when ``shutil.which`` finds\n        the binary, ``(False, \"not found on PATH\")`` otherwise. The\n        ``detail`` half is carried verbatim onto the\n        :class:`CapabilityStatus` row so ``eval doctor`` can print the\n        resolved path on success or the failure reason on miss.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        path = shutil.which(name)\n        if path is None:\n            return False, \"not found on PATH\"\n        return True, path\n\n    def mcp_server_reachable(self, name: str) -> ProbeResult:\n        \"\"\"Probe whether MCP server ``name`` is reachable.\n\n        OQ-5 deferral: the canonical reachability semantics (PATH\n        presence vs full stdio handshake vs SSE health probe) are open\n        and must resolve before COMP-009 closes at M2. Until then, this\n        implementation treats \"binary on PATH\" as the reachability\n        signal because every MCP server in the default roster\n        (``auggie``, ``auggie-mcp``, ``airis-mcp-gateway``) ships as an\n        executable.\n\n        Tests inject a custom probe via the ``mcp_probe`` constructor\n        argument; the M2 follow-up will land a real handshake probe\n        under the same signature.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._mcp_probe is not None:\n            return self._mcp_probe(name)\n        path = shutil.which(name)\n        if path is None:\n            return False, \"MCP server binary not on PATH\"\n        return True, path\n\n    def check_all(self) -> CapabilityReport:\n        \"\"\"Run every spec through its probe and assemble the report.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`CapabilityReport` whose ``report`` tuple has one\n            :class:`CapabilityStatus` row per spec in declaration order.\n            ``hard_failures`` / ``soft_skips`` / ``soft_xfails`` carry\n            the capability names that failed (or were skip-flagged) per\n            their declared ``failure_mode``. ``blocked_evals`` is empty\n            here — the SuiteLoader (T01.07) is the layer that knows\n            which evals depend on which capability and populates that\n            list at run time.\n\n            Calling :meth:`check_all` twice on the same gate instance\n            produces equal reports (idempotence is required by the\n            T01.11 acceptance criteria; verified by\n            ``test_check_all_is_idempotent``).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        rows: list[CapabilityStatus] = []\n        hard_failures: list[str] = []\n        soft_skips: list[str] = []\n        soft_xfails: list[str] = []\n\n        for spec in self._specs:\n            passed, detail = self._probe(spec)\n            skipped_by_flag = False\n            if (\n                spec.skip_flag is not None\n                and spec.skip_flag in self._skip_flags\n            ):\n                # SOFT-SKIP override: the user asked us to ignore this\n                # capability tier regardless of probe result. The probe\n                # detail is preserved so doctor output can still show\n                # why the capability would have passed/failed without\n                # the flag.\n                skipped_by_flag = True\n                passed = False\n\n            rows.append(\n                CapabilityStatus(\n                    name=spec.name,\n                    passed=passed,\n                    failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,\n                    description=spec.description,\n                    detail=detail,\n                    skipped_by_flag=skipped_by_flag,\n                )\n            )\n\n            if not passed:\n                if spec.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n                    hard_failures.append(spec.name)\n                elif spec.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n                    soft_skips.append(spec.name)\n                else:  # \"xfail\"\n                    soft_xfails.append(spec.name)\n\n        return CapabilityReport(\n            report=tuple(rows),\n            blocked_evals=(),\n            skip_flags=self.skip_flags,\n            hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n            soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n            soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _probe(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> ProbeResult:\n        if spec.kind == \"binary\":\n            return self.which_or_skip(spec.target)\n        if spec.kind == \"mcp_server\":\n            return self.mcp_server_reachable(spec.target)\n        # Defensive — the Literal annotation prevents this at type-check\n        # time, but the runtime guard keeps a typo in a custom spec\n        # tuple from silently producing an always-passing row.\n        raise ValueError(f\"unknown capability kind: {spec.kind!r}\")\n\n    def _make_capability(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> Capability:\n        if spec.kind == \"binary\":\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.which_or_skip(target)[0]\n        else:\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.mcp_server_reachable(target)[0]\n        return Capability(\n            name=spec.name,\n            check=_check,\n            failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,\n            skip_flag=spec.skip_flag,\n            description=spec.description,\n        )","originalFile":"\"\"\"Capability dataclasses for the cliEval harness.\n\n* ``Capability``        — DM-007 / D-0008 (Task T01.09): descriptor for a\n  single precondition the harness checks before running evals.\n* ``CapabilityStatus``  — DM-008 helper: per-capability outcome carried in\n  ``CapabilityReport.report``.\n* ``CapabilityReport``  — DM-008 / D-0009 (Task T01.10): aggregate report\n  produced by COMP-009 ``CapabilityGates.check_all`` and consumed by\n  ``eval doctor`` (T01.13) and the run pre-flight.\n\n``Capability`` 5-field contract (DM-007):\n\n* ``name``         — capability identifier (e.g. ``binary.claude``).\n* ``check``        — zero-arg callable returning ``True`` when the\n  capability is satisfied. ------------------------------------------------------------------","+    # internals","+    # ------------------------------------------------------------------","+","+    def _probe(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> ProbeResult:","+        if spec.kind == \"binary\":","+            return self.which_or_skip(spec.target)","+        if spec.kind == \"mcp_server\":","+            return self.mcp_server_reachable(spec.target)","+        # Defensive — the Literal annotation prevents this at type-check","+        # time, but the runtime guard keeps a typo in a custom spec","+        # tuple from silently producing an always-passing row.","+        raise ValueError(f\"unknown capability kind: {spec.kind!r}\")","+","+    def _make_capability(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> Capability:","+        if spec.kind == \"binary\":","+            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:","+                return self.which_or_skip(target)[0]","+        else:","+            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:","+                return self.mcp_server_reachable(target)[0]","+        return Capability(","+            name=spec.name,","+            check=_check,","+            failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,","+            skip_flag=spec.skip_flag,","+            description=spec.description,","+        )"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

Propagates onto `CapabilityReport.skip_flags`. |\n\n## Static binary roster (HARD / SOFT-SKIP semantics)\n\n| Capability `name` | `target` binary | `kind` | `failure_mode` | `skip_flag` | `description` |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `binary.claude` | `claude` | binary | `hard` | — | Claude CLI on PATH |\n| `binary.make` | `make` | binary | `hard` | — | GNU make on PATH |\n| `binary.jq` | `jq` | binary | `hard` | — | jq JSON processor on PATH |\n| `binary.git` | `git` | binary | `hard` | — | git VCS on PATH |\n| `mcp_server.auggie` | `auggie` | mcp_server | `skip` | `--no-mcp` | Auggie MCP server reachable |\n| `mcp_server.auggie-mcp` | `auggie-mcp` | mcp_server | `skip` | `--no-mcp` | auggie-mcp MCP server reachable |\n| `mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway` | `airis-mcp-gateway` | mcp_server | `skip` | `--no-mcp` | AIRIS MCP gateway reachable |\n\nRoster ordering matches design-spec §11 (HARD binaries first, then\nSOFT-SKIP MCP servers) so `eval doctor` (T01.13) renders a stable\ngreen-checklist layout.\n\n## HARD vs SOFT-SKIP semantics\n\n* **HARD** — `which_or_skip(target)` returns `(False, …)` →\n  `CapabilityStatus.passed = False`, name lands in\n  `CapabilityReport.hard_failures`. The doctor + run pre-flight MUST\n  exit 2 when `hard_failures` is non-empty (consumed in T01.13).\n* **SOFT-SKIP** — probe fails OR `skip_flag` is present in\n  `skip_flags` → `CapabilityStatus.passed = False`, name lands in\n  `CapabilityReport.soft_skips`. `skipped_by_flag` carries the override\n  bit so doctor can render \"would have passed at <path>, skipped by\n  --no-mcp\".\n* **SOFT-XFAIL** — reserved for manifest-driven `xfail_if:`\n  conditions; the gate honours `failure_mode=\"xfail\"` on custom spec\n  tuples (test coverage:\n  `test_xfail_capability_classifies_into_soft_xfails`). The default\n  roster does not declare any xfail capabilities.\n\n## `CapabilityReport` field population\n\n| Field | Populated by `check_all` | Note |\n|---|---|---|\n| `report` | one `CapabilityStatus` per spec in declaration order | Tuple, frozen, JSON-safe via `to_dict`. |\n| `blocked_evals` | empty | SuiteLoader (T01.07) populates this once it knows the eval-to-capability mapping. |\n| `skip_flags` | sorted, deduplicated `frozenset` | Mirrors the constructor input. |\n| `hard_failures` | names of HARD capabilities whose probe failed | Drives exit-2 from doctor/run pre-flight. |\n| `soft_skips` | names of SKIP capabilities whose probe failed or were flag-overridden | Reported but not fatal. |\n| `soft_xfails` | names of XFAIL capabilities whose probe failed | Reported but not fatal. |\n\n## OQ-5 deferral\n\n`mcp_server_reachable(name)` currently uses **PATH presence** as the\nreachability signal because every MCP server in the default roster\nships as an executable. The roadmap Open Question OQ-5 (\"Exact MCP\nserver reachability check semantics\") is targeted to resolve **before\nCOMP-009 close at M2**.\n\nThe override hook is the `mcp_probe` constructor keyword:\n\n```python\ngate = CapabilityGates(mcp_probe=lambda name: (passed, detail))\n```\n\nThe M2 follow-up will land a real handshake probe behind the same\nsignature so the gate's public surface does not change. Tests use this\nhook to simulate reachable / unreachable servers without touching\n`shutil`.\n\n## Invariants\n\n- `check_all()` is idempotent — repeated calls on the same instance\n  produce equal reports (covered by `test_check_all_is_idempotent`).\n- `CapabilityStatus` rows preserve declaration order so doctor output\n  is stable.\n- `skip_flags` are deduplicated and sorted before being emitted onto\n  the report.\n- Unknown capability kinds raise `ValueError` at `check_all()` time —\n  guards against typos in a custom spec tuple from silently producing\n  always-passing rows.\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- **`eval doctor`** (FR-CLI4 / T01.13) — calls `CapabilityGates().check_all().to_json()` for the `--json` output and renders `report[]` rows into the green checklist.\n- **`eval run` pre-flight** (M2) — inspects `hard_failures` to decide whether to abort (exit 2) and consumes `soft_skips` + `--no-mcp` to mark dependent evals SKIPPED.\n- **`SuiteLoader`** (T01.07) — does NOT currently inject `CapabilityGates`; uses the `PermissiveCapabilityResolver` stub. T01.13 wires the real gate through `commands.py`. Downstream may also adapt `CapabilityGates` to the `CapabilityResolver` protocol (`resolve(eval_id, required)`) when the loader → gate seam lands; the spec table provides the mapping needed for that.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.11) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| Class `CapabilityGates` exposes `check_all()`, `which_or_skip()`, `mcp_server_reachable()` and returns a populated `CapabilityReport`. | `capabilities.py` — `CapabilityGates` methods. |\n| Missing `claude` on PATH classifies HARD. | `_probe` → `which_or_skip` → `hard_failures`. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_0128iCVpDQj5byxuHdtk6UML","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01V1qM2u956cXknMpuTYf9R3","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0010/notes.md","content":"# D-0010 — Implementation notes\n\n## Design choices\n\n1. **Co-locate `CapabilityGates` with `Capability` / `CapabilityReport`\n   in `capabilities.py`.** Design-spec §3 proposed a separate\n   `capability_gates.py`, but T01.09 and T01.10 already established\n   `capabilities.py` as the module home for this subsystem. Splitting\n   the gate class into a sibling file would force every caller to\n   import from two modules and would introduce a circular-import risk\n   when `CapabilityGates._make_capability` materialises `Capability`\n   instances. Keeping everything in one file makes the\n   declaration → consumption flow readable top-to-bottom.\n\n2. **`_CapabilitySpec` as a private internal helper.** The `Capability`\n   dataclass (T01.09) only carries the per-capability *behaviour*\n   (`name`, `check` callable, `failure_mode`, `skip_flag`,\n   `description`). To probe a binary we also need the *target* string\n   (`claude` vs `binary.claude`) and the *kind* tag (`binary` vs\n   `mcp_server`) so the gate can pick the right probe. Stashing those\n   on a private `_CapabilitySpec` keeps `Capability` itself stable for\n   external callers and lets the static roster carry the extra metadata\n   without polluting the public type.\n\n3. **`mcp_probe` constructor hook for OQ-5.** Rather than wait for OQ-5\n   to land before shipping T01.11, the gate accepts an optional\n   `mcp_probe: Callable[[str], tuple[bool, str]]` keyword. Production\n   callers leave it `None` and get the M1 PATH-presence stub; the M2\n   patch (resolving OQ-5) swaps in a real handshake probe via the same\n   hook without touching `CapabilityGates`. Tests also use this hook to\n   simulate reachable/unreachable servers without monkeypatching\n   `shutil`.\n\n4. **`Capability.check` closures defer to gate methods.** The\n   `capabilities()` accessor materialises `Capability` instances whose\n   `check` closures call back into `self.which_or_skip` /\n   `self.mcp_server_reachable`. This guarantees there is exactly one\n   source of truth for what \"passed\" means — if a future patch tightens\n   `which_or_skip` (e.g., to enforce `is_executable`), the closures\n   automatically pick it up. The default-argument binding\n   (`target: str = spec.target`) inside the closure prevents the\n   classic late-binding-in-loop bug.\n\n5. **`blocked_evals` left empty by `check_all`.** The gate has no\n   knowledge of which evals depend on which capability — that mapping\n   lives on the `requires:` field of each `EvalSpec` in the parsed\n   suite. The SuiteLoader / runner is the layer that joins the two and\n   populates `blocked_evals`. Leaving the field empty here keeps the\n   single-responsibility separation clean and matches how the existing\n   `PermissiveCapabilityResolver` (T01.07) defers gate semantics to the\n   real implementation.\n\n6. **`skip_flags` sorted + deduplicated.** The constructor accepts any\n   iterable; the property emits a sorted tuple so two gates created\n   with the same flag set in different orders produce equal reports.\n   This is what gives `test_check_all_is_idempotent` its strict\n   equality guarantee.\n\n7. **Unknown-kind guard in `_probe`.** The `Literal[\"binary\",\n   \"mcp_server\"]` annotation on `_CapabilitySpec.kind` is a type-checker\n   hint, not a runtime constraint. A typo in a downstream custom spec\n   tuple (`kind=\"binaryy\"`) would otherwise silently fall through and\n   produce an always-passing row. The explicit `ValueError` raises at\n   `check_all` time so the test suite catches the typo via\n   `test_unknown_capability_kind_is_rejected_at_check_time`.\n\n## Trade-offs and follow-ups\n\n- **No `CapabilityResolver` adapter yet.** The SuiteLoader (T01.07)\n  consumes the `CapabilityResolver` protocol (`resolve(eval_id,\n  required)`). `CapabilityGates` does not implement that signature —\n  T01.13 will wire an adapter when it threads the real gate through\n  `commands.py`. Building the adapter now would be speculative: the\n  loader → gate seam wants to consult `CapabilityReport.report` to map\n  capability names to pass/fail, which the loader doesn't have access\n  to today.\n- **MCP reachability is intentionally weak.** The M1 stub treats\n  \"binary on PATH\" as the reachability signal. This passes locally\n  whenever the `auggie` CLI is installed but does NOT detect a wedged\n  server, an SSE endpoint behind a firewall, or an unreachable\n  gateway. OQ-5 must resolve before COMP-009 close at M2.\n- **No async / no timeout.** `shutil.which` is sub-millisecond on a\n  warm cache; a future MCP handshake probe will need a per-server\n  timeout. The `mcp_probe` hook keeps the seam in place for that\n  upgrade.\n- **`capabilities()` materialises new `Capability` instances on every\n  call.** This is intentional — the closures bind to the live gate\n  instance so a test that calls `gate.capabilities()[0].check()` sees\n  the same probe behaviour as `gate.check_all()`. The cost is trivial\n  (8 dataclass constructions); memoising would force us to invalidate\n  on `_mcp_probe` replacement.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3596,"cache_read_input_tokens":144841,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3596,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"293563b4-1ea3-4bc4-aee5-9bab90f1f262","uuid":"aa4d9bf8-1901-4037-a463-5d4f41444321"}
